<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that every admissible integrable minimizer of the zero-temperature Parisi functional for the pure, zero-field Sherrington–Kirkpatrick model has full relative Stieltjes support on $`[0,1)`$. Thus its support has no gaps at any overlap scale below one. We use the covariance normalization $`\xi(t)=t^2/2`$.

- **C01** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-full-support)): If $\gamma\in\mathcal U$ minimizes $\mathcal P$, then $$\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\mu_\gamma=[0,1).$$ In particular $\gamma(b)>\gamma(a)$ whenever $0\leq a<b<1$. For the solution of <a href="#eq:model-diffusion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:model-diffusion">[eq:model-diffusion]</a>, $$\label{eq:main-consistency} \mathbb E[u_\gamma(t,X_t)^2]=t,\qquad \mathbb E[\Phi_{\gamma,xx}(t,X_t)^2]=1, \qquad 0\leq t<1.$$
  - Explanation: Every admissible minimizer has full relative Stieltjes support and satisfies the gradient and Hessian moment identities.
- **C02** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-regularity)): For every $\gamma\in\mathcal U$, the function $\Phi_\gamma$ is even, convex, and one-Lipschitz in its spatial variable. It satisfies $$\|x\|\leq\Phi_\gamma(t,x) \leq \|x\|+\sqrt{\frac{2(1-t)}\pi} +\frac12\int_t^1\gamma(s)\,\mathrm ds. \label{eq:terminal-uniform}$$ For each integer $k\geq1$ and $T<1$, the derivative $\partial_x^k\Phi_\gamma$ is bounded and jointly continuous on $[0,T]\times\mathbb R$. These bounds are uniform when the coefficients have uniformly bounded $L^1(0,1)$ norms. The derivatives are locally absolutely continuous in time and satisfy the spatially differentiated Parisi equation for almost every time.* *If $\gamma_n\to\gamma$ in $L^1(0,1)$, then, for every $k\geq1$ and $T<1$, $$\sup_{[0,T]\times\mathbb R} \|\partial_x^k\Phi_{\gamma_n}-\partial_x^k\Phi_\gamma\| \longrightarrow0. \label{eq:derivative-convergence}$$ The same conclusion holds when each $\gamma_n$ is a finite step function and its terminal datum is $M_n^{-1}\log(2\cosh(M_nx))$, where $M_n\to\infty$.
  - Explanation: Spatial regularity and uniform convergence of derivatives on compact time strips survive integrable coefficient approximation and terminal smoothing.
- **C03** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-diffusion)): For $\gamma\in\mathcal U$, the equation $$\mathrm dX_t=\gamma(t)u_\gamma(t,X_t)\,\mathrm dt+\mathrm dB_t, \qquad X_0=0, \label{eq:controlled-diffusion}$$ has a unique strong solution on $[0,1]$. On every strip $[0,T]$ with $T<1$, $$u_\gamma(t,X_t)=\int_0^t \Phi_{\gamma,xx}(s,X_s)\,\mathrm dB_s. \label{eq:gradient-martingale}$$ In particular, $q(t)=\mathbb E[u_\gamma(t,X_t)^2]$ belongs to $C^1[0,1)$, $q(0)=0$, and $$q'(t)=\mathbb E[\Phi_{\gamma,xx}(t,X_t)^2]. \label{eq:qprime}$$ Under the approximations in Lemma <a href="#lem:regularity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:regularity">2</a>, the coupled diffusions converge uniformly on compact time strips, and $q_n$ and $q_n'$ converge uniformly there.
  - Explanation: The controlled diffusion is uniquely defined through terminal time; its gradient is a martingale and its squared moment has the stated Hessian derivative.
- **C04** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-constant-transfer)): Suppose $\gamma=c$ on an open interval $I\subset(0,1)$. If $c>0$, every point of $I$ has a neighborhood on which one may choose positive, bounded, nondecreasing finite step approximants equal to $c$, with smooth terminal data and $M_n\geq\\|\gamma_n\\|_\infty$. At each time in this neighborhood, all spatial derivatives and the corresponding diffusion expectations of bounded polynomials in those derivatives converge. Moreover, $\Phi_{\gamma,xx}>0$ throughout $I\times\mathbb R$.* *For $c>0$, set $r=c\Phi_x$, $v=r_x$, $w=r_{xx}$, and $z=r_{xxx}$. Then, within $I$, $$\begin{aligned} (q')'(t)&=c^{-2}\mathbb E[w(t,X_t)^2-2v(t,X_t)^3], \label{eq:first-curvature}\\ (q')''(t)&=c^{-2}\mathbb E[z(t,X_t)^2-12v(t,X_t)w(t,X_t)^2 +6v(t,X_t)^4]. \label{eq:second-curvature} \end{aligned}$$ For $c=0$, $q'$ is strictly increasing on $I$.
  - Explanation: Constant plateaus can be preserved in approximation, enabling derivative moment transfer and curvature differentiation. The vanishing coefficient case is treated separately.
- **C05** (bound, [source](#stmt-tail-class)): On each closed backward step, uniformly in its time coordinate, $$\label{tail:backward} l(t,x)=cx+C(t)+O(e^{-\kappa x})\qquad(x\to+\infty)$$ with differentiated exponential error. The functions are even, so the negative tail is determined by symmetry.* *For the forward heat factor $g$, on every closed forward step with time bounded away from zero, $$\label{tail:forward} g(t,x)=\exp\left(-\frac{x^2}{2t}+\beta(t)x+C_0(t)\right) (1+O(e^{-\kappa x}))\qquad(x\to+\infty),$$ with differentiated relative error. In particular, $$\label{tail:score} s(t,x)=\frac{x}{t}-\beta(t)+O(e^{-\kappa x}),\qquad s_x(t,x)=\frac1t+O(e^{-\kappa x}),\qquad s_{xx}(t,x)=O(e^{-\kappa x}).$$ The assertions include one-sided values at every step boundary.
  - Explanation: Differentiated exponential tails for the backward profile and Gaussian tails for the forward heat factor control spatial boundary terms.
- **C06** (theorem, [source](#stmt-shape-backward)): For the fixed step data above, $r$ is odd and $v>0$. On $x>0$, $$\label{shape:backward-signs} w\le0,\qquad H:=\frac{r_\tau}{r}=\frac{w}{2r}+v\le0, \qquad H_x\ge0.$$ The quotient defining $H$ extends smoothly and evenly at zero.
  - Explanation: The backward drift and its relative time change obey the shape signs needed for the coercive identity.
- **C07** (theorem, [source](#stmt-shape-forward)): For every $t>0$, $s$ is odd and, with $a=s_x$, $$\label{shape:forward-signs} a\ge\frac1t>0,\qquad s_{xx}\le0\quad(x>0),\qquad j:=a-\frac sr v=r\partial_x(s/r)\ge0\quad(x\in\mathbb R).$$ The ratios and $j$ extend smoothly at zero. At every forward step boundary, $j$ is unchanged.
  - Explanation: The forward heat score is increasing and concave on the positive half-line, and its ratio with the backward drift obeys the comparison sign.
- **C08** (theorem, [source](#stmt-shape-ibp)): At a fixed positive time in a constant step, the functions $R,K,v,b,j,L,B,S$ defined above are even and extend smoothly across zero. They satisfy $$R,K,v,j,L,B,S\ge0.$$ Writing $\mathcal D=r\partial_x$, one has $$\label{shape:D-rules} \mathcal D(R,K,v,b,j) =\bigl(2Rv,\ 2vK+Rj,\ -Rb,\ -z-bv,\ -jv+Kb-L\bigr).$$ For every polynomial $F$ in $R,K,v,b,j$, $$\label{shape:ibp-identity} \mathbb E_f\!\left[\mathcal DF+(v+R-K)F\right]=0.$$ Every term in this identity is absolutely integrable.
  - Explanation: Even certificate variables obey explicit derivative rules and an integrable divergence identity with zero expectation.
- **C09** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cert-identity-lemma)): With these definitions, $B=b-2v$, and $S=z+bv-2Rb$, one has the polynomial identity over $\mathbb Q$ $$\label{cert:identity} \begin{aligned} z^2-12Rvb^2+6v^4 ={}&(z+Q)^2+ST+\sum_{i=0}^4p_iP_i+\mathcal W(F_2)\\ &+L(x_0v^2+hbv)+P_{\mathrm{rem}}. \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: A rational polynomial identity decomposes the curvature expression into squares, nonnegative terms, and a divergence correction.
- **C10** (bound, [source](#stmt-cert-coercivity)): Suppose $r,v,w,z,s$ and the variables $R,K,b,j,L,B,S$ are related as in Lemma <a href="#shape:ibp" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="shape:ibp">8</a>, with the derivative rules <a href="#shape:D-rules" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="shape:D-rules">[shape:D-rules]</a> and signs <a href="#cert:signs" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cert:signs">[cert:signs]</a>. Assume their quotients extend across zero. Let $f$ be a probability density satisfying $f_x/f=r-s$ such that every term of <a href="#cert:identity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cert:identity">[cert:identity]</a> is integrable and $rfF_2$ tends to zero at both spatial infinities. Then $$\label{cert:inequality} \mathbb E_f[z^2-12vw^2+6v^4] \geq\frac{1661507}{1521000000}\mathbb E_fv^4 \geq\frac1{1000}\mathbb E_fv^4.$$
  - Explanation: Under the specified sign and boundary hypotheses, integrating the polynomial identity gives the coercive fourth-moment lower bound.
- **C11** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-gap-curvature)): Let $\gamma\in\mathcal U$ and suppose $\gamma=c$ on an open interval $I\subset(0,1)$. For its controlled diffusion, put $q(t)=\mathbb E\Phi_{\gamma,x}(t,X_t)^2$. If $c>0$, then $q'$ is strictly convex on $I$; more precisely, with $v=c\Phi_{\gamma,xx}$, $$\label{eq:strict-curvature} q'''(t)\geq\frac{1}{1000c^2}\mathbb Ev(t,X_t)^4>0, \qquad t\in I.$$ If $c=0$, then $q'$ is strictly increasing on $I$.
  - Explanation: Positive constant intervals have strictly convex gradient-moment derivative; vanishing constant intervals have strictly increasing derivative.
- **C12** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-first-variation)): Let $\gamma\in\mathcal U$, and suppose that $\eta\in L^1(0,1)$ has the property that $\gamma+\epsilon\eta\in\mathcal U$ for $0\leq\epsilon\leq\epsilon_0$, where $\epsilon_0>0$. With $X$ and $q$ from Lemma <a href="#lem:diffusion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:diffusion">3</a>, $$\left.\frac{\mathrm d}{\mathrm d\epsilon}\right\|_{\epsilon=0+} \mathcal P(\gamma+\epsilon\eta) =\frac12\int_0^1\eta(t)(q(t)-t)\,\mathrm dt. \label{eq:first-variation}$$
  - Explanation: The one-sided first variation is valid for every integrable direction preserving admissibility; the direction itself need not be nondecreasing.
- **C13** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-contact)): Let $\gamma$ minimize $\mathcal P$ over $\mathcal U$, and let $\mu$ be its locally finite Stieltjes measure on $[0,1)$, with the convention $\mu(\{0\})=\gamma(0)$. Thus $\gamma(t)=\mu([0,t])$. Define $$G(s)=\int_s^1(q(t)-t)\,\mathrm dt, \qquad S=\operatorname{supp}\mu\subset[0,1). \label{eq:contact-potential}$$ Then $G\geq0$ on $[0,1)$ and $G=0$ on $S$. At every $s\in S\cap(0,1)$, $$q(s)=s,\qquad q'(s)\leq1. \label{eq:contact-derivatives}$$ If $0\in S$, then also $q'(0)\leq1$, with the derivative from the right.
  - Explanation: Adding and removing Stieltjes mass yields a nonnegative contact potential vanishing on relative support and endpoint derivative inequalities.
- **C14** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-no-terminal-plateau)): For a minimizer $\gamma$, there is no $a<1$ and finite $c\geq0$ such that $\gamma=c$ on $(a,1)$.
  - Explanation: A boundary layer forces a contradiction to contact nonnegativity if a minimizing coefficient has a finite terminal plateau.
- **C15** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-smooth-measure)): Let $\gamma\in\mathcal U$ minimize $\mathcal P$, and let $X$ be its controlled diffusion. Then $\gamma(0)=0$ and $\gamma$ is smooth on $[0,1)$, with derivatives at zero understood from the right. Moreover, $$\label{eq:gamma-moment-ratio} \gamma(t)= \frac{\mathbb E[\Phi_{\gamma,xxx}(t,X_t)^2]} {2\mathbb E[\Phi_{\gamma,xx}(t,X_t)^3]}, \qquad 0\leq t<1.$$ Consequently $\mu_\gamma(\mathrm dt)=\gamma'(t)\,\mathrm dt$ on $[0,1)$, where $\gamma'$ is smooth and nonnegative. In fact, for $0<t<1$, $$\label{eq:positive-density-bound} 2\gamma'(t)\mathbb E[\Phi_{\gamma,xx}(t,X_t)^3] \geq \frac{\gamma(t)^2}{1000} \mathbb E[\Phi_{\gamma,xx}(t,X_t)^4]>0.$$ In particular $\gamma'(t)>0$ on $(0,1)$.
  - Explanation: Saturated consistency identifies the minimizer as a smooth derivative-moment ratio; its Stieltjes density is positive away from the left endpoint.
- **C16** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-value)): Let $\gamma$ be a minimizer, let $X$ be its diffusion, and write $u=\Phi_{\gamma,x}$ and $a=\Phi_{\gamma,xx}$. There is a bounded martingale terminal value $U_1$ such that $u(t,X_t)\to U_1$ almost surely and in $L^2$ as $t\uparrow1$, with $U_1^2=1$ almost surely. Moreover, $$\label{eq:value-identities} \begin{aligned} P_*&=\mathbb E\|X_1\|-\int_0^1t\gamma(t)\,\mathrm dt\\ &=\mathbb E[U_1 B_1] =\int_0^1\mathbb Ea(t,X_t)\,\mathrm dt. \end{aligned}$$ The last integral is finite.
  - Explanation: The terminal gradient martingale represents the ground-state value through Brownian correlation and expected Hessian covariation.
- **C17** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-gaussian)): Fix $0<T<1$, a minimizer $\gamma$, and $u,a$ as above. For an integer $N\geq1$ put $\Delta=T/N$ and $t_j=j\Delta$. On independent standard normal variables $Z_1,\ldots,Z_{N+1}$, define $$\begin{aligned} Y_0&=0,\qquad Y_j=Y_{j-1}+\sqrt\Delta Z_j +\Delta\gamma(t_{j-1})u(t_{j-1},Y_{j-1}), &&1\leq j\leq N,\label{eq:euler}\\ c_j&=\bigl(\mathbb Ea(t_{j-1},Y_{j-1})^2\bigr)^{-1/2}>0, \qquad g_j=c_j a(t_{j-1},Y_{j-1})Z_j. \label{eq:normalized-increment} \end{aligned}$$ For all sufficiently large $N$ these constants are finite and positive. For these mesh counts, let $F_{\rm G}$ denote the standard normal distribution function and set $$\begin{gathered} F_N=\operatorname{sgn}\bigl(u(T,Y_N)+2F_{\rm G}(Z_{N+1})-1\bigr),\\ A_j=\mathbb E[F_Ng_j],\qquad B_i=\mathbb E[F_NZ_i],\qquad A_0=0. \end{gathered}$$ Here $1\leq j\leq N$ and $1\leq i\leq N+1$ in the definitions of $A_j$ and $B_i$. The value of the sign at zero is immaterial. Then $$\label{eq:finite-value} \lim_{N\to\infty}\sum_{j=0}^N A_jB_{j+1} =\int_0^T\mathbb Ea(t,X_t)\,\mathrm dt.$$ Letting $T\uparrow1$ on the right gives $P_*$.
  - Explanation: Normalized Gaussian Euler increments and randomized rounding yield a scalar adjacent-correlation approximation to the value integral.
- **C18** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-cor-algorithm)): For every fixed $\epsilon>0$, there is a randomized algorithm with operation count at most $C(\epsilon)n^2$ in the model just described, where $C(\epsilon)$ is independent of $n$, that takes the Gaussian couplings of $H_n$ as input and returns $\sigma^{\rm alg}\in\{-1,1\}^n$ such that $$\Pr\left\{ H_n(\sigma^{\rm alg})\geq \max_{\sigma\in\{-1,1\}^n}H_n(\sigma)-\epsilon n \right\}\longrightarrow1 \qquad(n\to\infty).$$ The probability includes the couplings and the algorithm’s randomness.
  - Explanation: The full-support conclusion verifies the hypothesis of an existing asymptotic randomized optimization theorem in the stated real-arithmetic model.

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-regularity",
        "exact_quote": "For every $\\gamma\\in\\mathcal U$, the function $\\Phi_\\gamma$ is even, convex, and one-Lipschitz in its spatial variable. It satisfies $$|x|\\leq\\Phi_\\gamma(t,x)\n \\leq |x|+\\sqrt{\\frac{2(1-t)}\\pi}\n               +\\frac12\\int_t^1\\gamma(s)\\,\\mathrm ds.\n \\label{eq:terminal-uniform}$$ For each integer $k\\geq1$ and $T<1$, the derivative $\\partial_x^k\\Phi_\\gamma$ is bounded and jointly continuous on $[0,T]\\times\\mathbb R$. These bounds are uniform when the coefficients have uniformly bounded $L^1(0,1)$ norms. The derivatives are locally absolutely continuous in time and satisfy the spatially differentiated Parisi equation for almost every time.*\n\n*If $\\gamma_n\\to\\gamma$ in $L^1(0,1)$, then, for every $k\\geq1$ and $T<1$, $$\\sup_{[0,T]\\times\\mathbb R}\n |\\partial_x^k\\Phi_{\\gamma_n}-\\partial_x^k\\Phi_\\gamma|\n \\longrightarrow0.\n \\label{eq:derivative-convergence}$$ The same conclusion holds when each $\\gamma_n$ is a finite step function and its terminal datum is $M_n^{-1}\\log(2\\cosh(M_nx))$, where $M_n\\to\\infty$.",
        "source_id": "PM-281.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-regularity",
          "exact_quote": "For every $\\gamma\\in\\mathcal U$, the function $\\Phi_\\gamma$ is even, convex, and one-Lipschitz in its spatial variable. These bounds are uniform when the coefficients have uniformly bounded $L^1(0,1)$ norms. The derivatives are locally absolutely continuous in time and satisfy the spatially differentiated Parisi equation for almost every time.*\n\n*If $\\gamma_n\\to\\gamma$ in $L^1(0,1)$, then, for every $k\\geq1$ and $T<1$, $$\\sup_{[0,T]\\times\\mathbb R}\n |\\partial_x^k\\Phi_{\\gamma_n}-\\partial_x^k\\Phi_\\gamma|\n \\longrightarrow0.\n \\label{eq:derivative-convergence}$$ The same conclusion holds when each $\\gamma_n$ is a finite step function and its terminal datum is $M_n^{-1}\\log(2\\cosh(M_nx))$, where $M_n\\to\\infty$.",
          "source_id": "PM-281.2"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-diffusion",
        "exact_quote": "For $\\gamma\\in\\mathcal U$, the equation $$\\mathrm dX_t=\\gamma(t)u_\\gamma(t,X_t)\\,\\mathrm dt+\\mathrm dB_t,\n \\qquad X_0=0,\n \\label{eq:controlled-diffusion}$$ has a unique strong solution on $[0,1]$. On every strip $[0,T]$ with $T<1$, $$u_\\gamma(t,X_t)=\\int_0^t\n                \\Phi_{\\gamma,xx}(s,X_s)\\,\\mathrm dB_s.\n \\label{eq:gradient-martingale}$$ In particular, $q(t)=\\mathbb E[u_\\gamma(t,X_t)^2]$ belongs to $C^1[0,1)$, $q(0)=0$, and $$q'(t)=\\mathbb E[\\Phi_{\\gamma,xx}(t,X_t)^2].\n \\label{eq:qprime}$$ Under the approximations in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:regularity\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:regularity\">2</a>, the coupled diffusions converge uniformly on compact time strips, and $q_n$ and $q_n'$ converge uniformly there.",
        "source_id": "PM-281.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-diffusion",
          "exact_quote": "For $\\gamma\\in\\mathcal U$, the equation $$\\mathrm dX_t=\\gamma(t)u_\\gamma(t,X_t)\\,\\mathrm dt+\\mathrm dB_t,\n \\qquad X_0=0,\n \\label{eq:controlled-diffusion}$$ has a unique strong solution on $[0,1]$. On every strip $[0,T]$ with $T<1$, $$u_\\gamma(t,X_t)=\\int_0^t\n                \\Phi_{\\gamma,xx}(s,X_s)\\,\\mathrm dB_s.\n \\label{eq:gradient-martingale}$$ In particular, $q(t)=\\mathbb E[u_\\gamma(t,X_t)^2]$ belongs to $C^1[0,1)$, $q(0)=0$, and $$q'(t)=\\mathbb E[\\Phi_{\\gamma,xx}(t,X_t)^2].\n \\label{eq:qprime}$$ Under the approximations in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:regularity\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:regularity\">2</a>, the coupled diffusions converge uniformly on compact time strips, and $q_n$ and $q_n'$ converge uniformly there.",
          "source_id": "PM-281.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For $\\gamma\\in\\mathcal U$, the equation $$\\mathrm dX_t=\\gamma(t)u_\\gamma(t,X_t)\\,\\mathrm dt+\\mathrm dB_t,\n \\qquad X_0=0,\n \\label{eq:controlled-diffusion}$$ has a unique strong solution on $[0,1]$. On every strip $[0,T]$ with $T<1$, $$u_\\gamma(t,X_t)=\\int_0^t\n                \\Phi_{\\gamma,xx}(s,X_s)\\,\\mathrm dB_s.\n \\label{eq:gradient-martingale}$$ In particular, $q(t)=\\mathbb E[u_\\gamma(t,X_t)^2]$ belongs to $C^1[0,1)$, $q(0)=0$, and $$q'(t)=\\mathbb E[\\Phi_{\\gamma,xx}(t,X_t)^2].\n \\label{eq:qprime}$$ Under the approximations in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:regularity\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:regularity\">2</a>, the coupled diffusions converge uniformly on compact time strips, and $q_n$ and $q_n'$ converge uniformly there.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-constant-transfer",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $\\gamma=c$ on an open interval $I\\subset(0,1)$. If $c>0$, every point of $I$ has a neighborhood on which one may choose positive, bounded, nondecreasing finite step approximants equal to $c$, with smooth terminal data and $M_n\\geq\\|\\gamma_n\\|_\\infty$. At each time in this neighborhood, all spatial derivatives and the corresponding diffusion expectations of bounded polynomials in those derivatives converge. Moreover, $\\Phi_{\\gamma,xx}>0$ throughout $I\\times\\mathbb R$.*\n\n\n\n*For $c>0$, set $r=c\\Phi_x$, $v=r_x$, $w=r_{xx}$, and $z=r_{xxx}$. Then, within $I$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n (q')'(t)&=c^{-2}\\mathbb E[w(t,X_t)^2-2v(t,X_t)^3],\n \\label{eq:first-curvature}\\\\\n (q')''(t)&=c^{-2}\\mathbb E[z(t,X_t)^2-12v(t,X_t)w(t,X_t)^2\n                                      +6v(t,X_t)^4].\n \\label{eq:second-curvature}\n\\end{aligned}$$ For $c=0$, $q'$ is strictly increasing on $I$.",
        "source_id": "PM-281.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-constant-transfer",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose $\\gamma=c$ on an open interval $I\\subset(0,1)$. At each time in this neighborhood, all spatial derivatives and the corresponding diffusion expectations of bounded polynomials in those derivatives converge. Moreover, $\\Phi_{\\gamma,xx}>0$ throughout $I\\times\\mathbb R$.*\n\n\n\n*For $c>0$, set $r=c\\Phi_x$, $v=r_x$, $w=r_{xx}$, and $z=r_{xxx}$. Then, within $I$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n (q')'(t)&=c^{-2}\\mathbb E[w(t,X_t)^2-2v(t,X_t)^3],\n \\label{eq:first-curvature}\\\\\n (q')''(t)&=c^{-2}\\mathbb E[z(t,X_t)^2-12v(t,X_t)w(t,X_t)^2\n                                      +6v(t,X_t)^4].\n \\label{eq:second-curvature}\n\\end{aligned}$$ For $c=0$, $q'$ is strictly increasing on $I$.",
          "source_id": "PM-281.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Suppose $\\gamma=c$ on an open interval $I\\subset(0,1)$. At each time in this neighborhood, all spatial derivatives and the corresponding diffusion expectations of bounded polynomials in those derivatives converge. Moreover, $\\Phi_{\\gamma,xx}>0$ throughout $I\\times\\mathbb R$.*\n\n\n\n*For $c>0$, set $r=c\\Phi_x$, $v=r_x$, $w=r_{xx}$, and $z=r_{xxx}$. Then, within $I$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n (q')'(t)&=c^{-2}\\mathbb E[w(t,X_t)^2-2v(t,X_t)^3],\n \\label{eq:first-curvature}\\\\\n (q')''(t)&=c^{-2}\\mathbb E[z(t,X_t)^2-12v(t,X_t)w(t,X_t)^2\n                                      +6v(t,X_t)^4].\n \\label{eq:second-curvature}\n\\end{aligned}$$ For $c=0$, $q'$ is strictly increasing on $I$.",
      "explanation": "Constant plateaus can be preserved in approximation, enabling derivative moment transfer and curvature differentiation. The vanishing coefficient case is treated separately.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-tail-class",
        "exact_quote": "On each closed backward step, uniformly in its time coordinate, $$\\label{tail:backward}\n l(t,x)=cx+C(t)+O(e^{-\\kappa x})\\qquad(x\\to+\\infty)$$ with differentiated exponential error. The functions are even, so the negative tail is determined by symmetry.*\n\n\n\n*For the forward heat factor $g$, on every closed forward step with time bounded away from zero, $$\\label{tail:forward}\n g(t,x)=\\exp\\left(-\\frac{x^2}{2t}+\\beta(t)x+C_0(t)\\right)\n                 (1+O(e^{-\\kappa x}))\\qquad(x\\to+\\infty),$$ with differentiated relative error. In particular, $$\\label{tail:score}\n s(t,x)=\\frac{x}{t}-\\beta(t)+O(e^{-\\kappa x}),\\qquad\n s_x(t,x)=\\frac1t+O(e^{-\\kappa x}),\\qquad\n s_{xx}(t,x)=O(e^{-\\kappa x}).$$ The assertions include one-sided values at every step boundary.",
        "source_id": "PM-281.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-tail-class",
          "exact_quote": "On each closed backward step, uniformly in its time coordinate, $$\\label{tail:backward}\n l(t,x)=cx+C(t)+O(e^{-\\kappa x})\\qquad(x\\to+\\infty)$$ with differentiated exponential error. The functions are even, so the negative tail is determined by symmetry.*\n\n\n\n*For the forward heat factor $g$, on every closed forward step with time bounded away from zero, $$\\label{tail:forward}\n g(t,x)=\\exp\\left(-\\frac{x^2}{2t}+\\beta(t)x+C_0(t)\\right)\n                 (1+O(e^{-\\kappa x}))\\qquad(x\\to+\\infty),$$ with differentiated relative error. In particular, $$\\label{tail:score}\n s(t,x)=\\frac{x}{t}-\\beta(t)+O(e^{-\\kappa x}),\\qquad\n s_x(t,x)=\\frac1t+O(e^{-\\kappa x}),\\qquad\n s_{xx}(t,x)=O(e^{-\\kappa x}).$$ The assertions include one-sided values at every step boundary.",
          "source_id": "PM-281.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "On each closed backward step, uniformly in its time coordinate, $$\\label{tail:backward}\n l(t,x)=cx+C(t)+O(e^{-\\kappa x})\\qquad(x\\to+\\infty)$$ with differentiated exponential error.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-shape-ibp",
        "exact_quote": "At a fixed positive time in a constant step, the functions $R,K,v,b,j,L,B,S$ defined above are even and extend smoothly across zero. They satisfy $$R,K,v,j,L,B,S\\ge0.$$ Writing $\\mathcal D=r\\partial_x$, one has $$\\label{shape:D-rules}\n \\mathcal D(R,K,v,b,j)\n  =\\bigl(2Rv,\\ 2vK+Rj,\\ -Rb,\\ -z-bv,\\ -jv+Kb-L\\bigr).$$ For every polynomial $F$ in $R,K,v,b,j$, $$\\label{shape:ibp-identity}\n \\mathbb E_f\\!\\left[\\mathcal DF+(v+R-K)F\\right]=0.$$ Every term in this identity is absolutely integrable.",
        "source_id": "PM-281.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-shape-ibp",
          "exact_quote": "At a fixed positive time in a constant step, the functions $R,K,v,b,j,L,B,S$ defined above are even and extend smoothly across zero.           "source_id": "PM-281.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "At a fixed positive time in a constant step, the functions $R,K,v,b,j,L,B,S$ defined above are even and extend smoothly across zero. They satisfy $$R,K,v,j,L,B,S\\ge0.$$ Writing $\\mathcal D=r\\partial_x$, one has $$\\label{shape:D-rules}\n \\mathcal D(R,K,v,b,j)\n  =\\bigl(2Rv,\\ 2vK+Rj,\\ -Rb,\\ -z-bv,\\ -jv+Kb-L\\bigr).$$ For every polynomial $F$ in $R,K,v,b,j$, $$\\label{shape:ibp-identity}\n \\mathbb E_f\\!\\left[\\mathcal DF+(v+R-K)F\\right]=0.$$ Every term in this identity is absolutely integrable.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cert-identity-lemma",
        "exact_quote": "With these definitions, $B=b-2v$, and $S=z+bv-2Rb$, one has the polynomial identity over $\\mathbb Q$ $$\\label{cert:identity}\n\\begin{aligned}\n z^2-12Rvb^2+6v^4\n  ={}&(z+Q)^2+ST+\\sum_{i=0}^4p_iP_i+\\mathcal W(F_2)\\\\\n     &+L(x_0v^2+hbv)+P_{\\mathrm{rem}}.\n\\end{aligned}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-281.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cert-identity-lemma",
          "exact_quote": "With these definitions, $B=b-2v$, and $S=z+bv-2Rb$, one has the polynomial identity over $\\mathbb Q$ $$\\label{cert:identity}\n\\begin{aligned}\n z^2-12Rvb^2+6v^4\n  ={}&(z+Q)^2+ST+\\sum_{i=0}^4p_iP_i+\\mathcal W(F_2)\\\\\n     &+L(x_0v^2+hbv)+P_{\\mathrm{rem}}.\n\\end{aligned}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-281.2"
        }

      "exact_statement": "With these definitions, $B=b-2v$, and $S=z+bv-2Rb$, one has the polynomial identity over $\\mathbb Q$ $$\\label{cert:identity}\n\\begin{aligned}\n z^2-12Rvb^2+6v^4\n  ={}&(z+Q)^2+ST+\\sum_{i=0}^4p_iP_i+\\mathcal W(F_2)\\\\\n     &+L(x_0v^2+hbv)+P_{\\mathrm{rem}}.\n\\end{aligned}$$",
      "explanation": "A rational polynomial identity decomposes the curvature expression into squares, nonnegative terms, and a divergence correction.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cert-coercivity",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $r,v,w,z,s$ and the variables $R,K,b,j,L,B,S$ are related as in Lemma <a href=\"#shape:ibp\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"shape:ibp\">8</a>, with the derivative rules <a href=\"#shape:D-rules\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"shape:D-rules\">[shape:D-rules]</a> and signs <a href=\"#cert:signs\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cert:signs\">[cert:signs]</a>. Assume their quotients extend across zero. Let $f$ be a probability density satisfying $f_x/f=r-s$ such that every term of <a href=\"#cert:identity\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cert:identity\">[cert:identity]</a> is integrable and $rfF_2$ tends to zero at both spatial infinities. Then $$\\label{cert:inequality}\n \\mathbb E_f[z^2-12vw^2+6v^4]\n \\geq\\frac{1661507}{1521000000}\\mathbb E_fv^4\n \\geq\\frac1{1000}\\mathbb E_fv^4.$$",

      },

Then $$\\label{cert:inequality}\n \\mathbb E_f[z^2-12vw^2+6v^4]\n \\geq\\frac{1661507}{1521000000}\\mathbb E_fv^4\n \\geq\\frac1{1000}\\mathbb E_fv^4.$$",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-gap-curvature",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\gamma\\in\\mathcal U$ and suppose $\\gamma=c$ on an open interval $I\\subset(0,1)$. For its controlled diffusion, put $q(t)=\\mathbb E\\Phi_{\\gamma,x}(t,X_t)^2$. If $c>0$, then $q'$ is strictly convex on $I$; more precisely, with $v=c\\Phi_{\\gamma,xx}$, $$\\label{eq:strict-curvature}\n q'''(t)\\geq\\frac{1}{1000c^2}\\mathbb Ev(t,X_t)^4>0,\n \\qquad t\\in I.$$ If $c=0$, then $q'$ is strictly increasing on $I$.",
        "source_id": "PM-281.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-gap-curvature",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\gamma\\in\\mathcal U$ and suppose $\\gamma=c$ on an open interval $I\\subset(0,1)$. For its controlled diffusion, put $q(t)=\\mathbb E\\Phi_{\\gamma,x}(t,X_t)^2$.           "source_id": "PM-281.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $\\gamma\\in\\mathcal U$ and suppose $\\gamma=c$ on an open interval $I\\subset(0,1)$. For its controlled diffusion, put $q(t)=\\mathbb E\\Phi_{\\gamma,x}(t,X_t)^2$.       "explanation": "Positive constant intervals have strictly convex gradient-moment derivative; vanishing constant intervals have strictly increasing derivative.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-first-variation",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\gamma\\in\\mathcal U$, and suppose that $\\eta\\in L^1(0,1)$ has the property that $\\gamma+\\epsilon\\eta\\in\\mathcal U$ for $0\\leq\\epsilon\\leq\\epsilon_0$, where $\\epsilon_0>0$. With $X$ and $q$ from Lemma <a href=\"#lem:diffusion\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:diffusion\">3</a>, $$\\left.\\frac{\\mathrm d}{\\mathrm d\\epsilon}\\right|_{\\epsilon=0+}\n       \\mathcal P(\\gamma+\\epsilon\\eta)\n   =\\frac12\\int_0^1\\eta(t)(q(t)-t)\\,\\mathrm dt.\n \\label{eq:first-variation}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-281.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-first-variation",

          "source_id": "PM-281.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $\\gamma\\in\\mathcal U$, and suppose that $\\eta\\in L^1(0,1)$ has the property that $\\gamma+\\epsilon\\eta\\in\\mathcal U$ for $0\\leq\\epsilon\\leq\\epsilon_0$, where $\\epsilon_0>0$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-contact",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\gamma$ minimize $\\mathcal P$ over $\\mathcal U$, and let $\\mu$ be its locally finite Stieltjes measure on $[0,1)$, with the convention $\\mu(\\{0\\})=\\gamma(0)$. Thus $\\gamma(t)=\\mu([0,t])$. Define $$G(s)=\\int_s^1(q(t)-t)\\,\\mathrm dt,\n \\qquad S=\\operatorname{supp}\\mu\\subset[0,1).\n \\label{eq:contact-potential}$$ Then $G\\geq0$ on $[0,1)$ and $G=0$ on $S$. At every $s\\in S\\cap(0,1)$, $$q(s)=s,\\qquad q'(s)\\leq1.\n \\label{eq:contact-derivatives}$$ If $0\\in S$, then also $q'(0)\\leq1$, with the derivative from the right.",
        "source_id": "PM-281.2"

At every $s\\in S\\cap(0,1)$, $$q(s)=s,\\qquad q'(s)\\leq1.\n \\label{eq:contact-derivatives}$$ If $0\\in S$, then also $q'(0)\\leq1$, with the derivative from the right.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-smooth-measure",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\gamma\\in\\mathcal U$ minimize $\\mathcal P$, and let $X$ be its controlled diffusion. Then $\\gamma(0)=0$ and $\\gamma$ is smooth on $[0,1)$, with derivatives at zero understood from the right. Moreover, $$\\label{eq:gamma-moment-ratio}\n \\gamma(t)=\n \\frac{\\mathbb E[\\Phi_{\\gamma,xxx}(t,X_t)^2]}\n      {2\\mathbb E[\\Phi_{\\gamma,xx}(t,X_t)^3]},\n \\qquad 0\\leq t<1.$$ Consequently $\\mu_\\gamma(\\mathrm dt)=\\gamma'(t)\\,\\mathrm dt$ on $[0,1)$, where $\\gamma'$ is smooth and nonnegative. In fact, for $0<t<1$, $$\\label{eq:positive-density-bound}\n 2\\gamma'(t)\\mathbb E[\\Phi_{\\gamma,xx}(t,X_t)^3]\n \\geq \\frac{\\gamma(t)^2}{1000}\n                \\mathbb E[\\Phi_{\\gamma,xx}(t,X_t)^4]>0.$$ In particular $\\gamma'(t)>0$ on $(0,1)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-281.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-smooth-measure",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\gamma\\in\\mathcal U$ minimize $\\mathcal P$, and let $X$ be its controlled diffusion. Then $\\gamma(0)=0$ and $\\gamma$ is smooth on $[0,1)$, with derivatives at zero understood from the right. Moreover, $$\\label{eq:gamma-moment-ratio}\n \\gamma(t)=\n \\frac{\\mathbb E[\\Phi_{\\gamma,xxx}(t,X_t)^2]}\n      {2\\mathbb E[\\Phi_{\\gamma,xx}(t,X_t)^3]},\n \\qquad 0\\leq t<1.$$ Consequently $\\mu_\\gamma(\\mathrm dt)=\\gamma'(t)\\,\\mathrm dt$ on $[0,1)$, where $\\gamma'$ is smooth and nonnegative. In fact, for $0<t<1$, $$\\label{eq:positive-density-bound}\n 2\\gamma'(t)\\mathbb E[\\Phi_{\\gamma,xx}(t,X_t)^3]\n \\geq \\frac{\\gamma(t)^2}{1000}\n                \\mathbb E[\\Phi_{\\gamma,xx}(t,X_t)^4]>0.$$ In particular $\\gamma'(t)>0$ on $(0,1)$.",
          "source_id": "PM-281.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $\\gamma\\in\\mathcal U$ minimize $\\mathcal P$, and let $X$ be its controlled diffusion. Moreover, $$\\label{eq:gamma-moment-ratio}\n \\gamma(t)=\n \\frac{\\mathbb E[\\Phi_{\\gamma,xxx}(t,X_t)^2]}\n      {2\\mathbb E[\\Phi_{\\gamma,xx}(t,X_t)^3]},\n \\qquad 0\\leq t<1.$$ Consequently $\\mu_\\gamma(\\mathrm dt)=\\gamma'(t)\\,\\mathrm dt$ on $[0,1)$, where $\\gamma'$ is smooth and nonnegative. In fact, for $0<t<1$, $$\\label{eq:positive-density-bound}\n 2\\gamma'(t)\\mathbb E[\\Phi_{\\gamma,xx}(t,X_t)^3]\n \\geq \\frac{\\gamma(t)^2}{1000}\n                \\mathbb E[\\Phi_{\\gamma,xx}(t,X_t)^4]>0.$$ In particular $\\gamma'(t)>0$ on $(0,1)$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-value",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\gamma$ be a minimizer, let $X$ be its diffusion, and write $u=\\Phi_{\\gamma,x}$ and $a=\\Phi_{\\gamma,xx}$. There is a bounded martingale terminal value $U_1$ such that $u(t,X_t)\\to U_1$ almost surely and in $L^2$ as $t\\uparrow1$, with $U_1^2=1$ almost surely. Moreover, $$\\label{eq:value-identities}\n \\begin{aligned}\n P_*&=\\mathbb E|X_1|-\\int_0^1t\\gamma(t)\\,\\mathrm dt\\\\\n    &=\\mathbb E[U_1 B_1]\n     =\\int_0^1\\mathbb Ea(t,X_t)\\,\\mathrm dt.\n \\end{aligned}$$ The last integral is finite.",
        "source_id": "PM-281.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-value",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\gamma$ be a minimizer, let $X$ be its diffusion, and write $u=\\Phi_{\\gamma,x}$ and $a=\\Phi_{\\gamma,xx}$. There is a bounded martingale terminal value $U_1$ such that $u(t,X_t)\\to U_1$ almost surely and in $L^2$ as $t\\uparrow1$, with $U_1^2=1$ almost surely. Moreover, $$\\label{eq:value-identities}\n \\begin{aligned}\n P_*&=\\mathbb E|X_1|-\\int_0^1t\\gamma(t)\\,\\mathrm dt\\\\\n    &=\\mathbb E[U_1 B_1]\n     =\\int_0^1\\mathbb Ea(t,X_t)\\,\\mathrm dt.\n \\end{aligned}$$ The last integral is finite.",
          "source_id": "PM-281.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $\\gamma$ be a minimizer, let $X$ be its diffusion, and write $u=\\Phi_{\\gamma,x}$ and $a=\\Phi_{\\gamma,xx}$. There is a bounded martingale terminal value $U_1$ such that $u(t,X_t)\\to U_1$ almost surely and in $L^2$ as $t\\uparrow1$, with $U_1^2=1$ almost surely. Moreover, $$\\label{eq:value-identities}\n \\begin{aligned}\n P_*&=\\mathbb E|X_1|-\\int_0^1t\\gamma(t)\\,\\mathrm dt\\\\\n    &=\\mathbb E[U_1 B_1]\n     =\\int_0^1\\mathbb Ea(t,X_t)\\,\\mathrm dt.\n \\end{aligned}$$ The last integral is finite.",
      "explanation": "The terminal gradient martingale represents the ground-state value through Brownian correlation and expected Hessian covariation.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-value",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\gamma$ be a minimizer, let $X$ be its diffusion, and write $u=\\Phi_{\\gamma,x}$ and $a=\\Phi_{\\gamma,xx}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-281.2"

      ],
      "id": "C16",
      "kind": "theorem",
      "quantifiers": [],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-cor-value"
    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-gaussian",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $0<T<1$, a minimizer $\\gamma$, and $u,a$ as above. For an integer $N\\geq1$ put $\\Delta=T/N$ and $t_j=j\\Delta$. On independent standard normal variables $Z_1,\\ldots,Z_{N+1}$, define $$\\begin{aligned}\n Y_0&=0,\\qquad\n Y_j=Y_{j-1}+\\sqrt\\Delta Z_j\n      +\\Delta\\gamma(t_{j-1})u(t_{j-1},Y_{j-1}),\n       &&1\\leq j\\leq N,\\label{eq:euler}\\\\\n c_j&=\\bigl(\\mathbb Ea(t_{j-1},Y_{j-1})^2\\bigr)^{-1/2}>0,\n \\qquad g_j=c_j a(t_{j-1},Y_{j-1})Z_j.\n       \\label{eq:normalized-increment}\n\\end{aligned}$$ For all sufficiently large $N$ these constants are finite and positive. For these mesh counts, let $F_{\\rm G}$ denote the standard normal distribution function and set $$\\begin{gathered}\n F_N=\\operatorname{sgn}\\bigl(u(T,Y_N)+2F_{\\rm G}(Z_{N+1})-1\\bigr),\\\\\n A_j=\\mathbb E[F_Ng_j],\\qquad B_i=\\mathbb E[F_NZ_i],\\qquad A_0=0.\n \\end{gathered}$$ Here $1\\leq j\\leq N$ and $1\\leq i\\leq N+1$ in the definitions of $A_j$ and $B_i$. The value of the sign at zero is immaterial. Then $$\\label{eq:finite-value}\n \\lim_{N\\to\\infty}\\sum_{j=0}^N A_jB_{j+1}\n       =\\int_0^T\\mathbb Ea(t,X_t)\\,\\mathrm dt.$$ Letting $T\\uparrow1$ on the right gives $P_*$.",
        "source_id": "PM-281.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-gaussian",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $0<T<1$, a minimizer $\\gamma$, and $u,a$ as above. For an integer $N\\geq1$ put $\\Delta=T/N$ and $t_j=j\\Delta$. On independent standard normal variables $Z_1,\\ldots,Z_{N+1}$, define $$\\begin{aligned}\n Y_0&=0,\\qquad\n Y_j=Y_{j-1}+\\sqrt\\Delta Z_j\n      +\\Delta\\gamma(t_{j-1})u(t_{j-1},Y_{j-1}),\n       &&1\\leq j\\leq N,\\label{eq:euler}\\\\\n c_j&=\\bigl(\\mathbb Ea(t_{j-1},Y_{j-1})^2\\bigr)^{-1/2}>0,\n \\qquad g_j=c_j a(t_{j-1},Y_{j-1})Z_j.\n       \\label{eq:normalized-increment}\n\\end{aligned}$$ For all sufficiently large $N$ these constants are finite and positive. For these mesh counts, let $F_{\\rm G}$ denote the standard normal distribution function and set $$\\begin{gathered}\n F_N=\\operatorname{sgn}\\bigl(u(T,Y_N)+2F_{\\rm G}(Z_{N+1})-1\\bigr),\\\\\n A_j=\\mathbb E[F_Ng_j],\\qquad B_i=\\mathbb E[F_NZ_i],\\qquad A_0=0.\n \\end{gathered}$$ Here $1\\leq j\\leq N$ and $1\\leq i\\leq N+1$ in the definitions of $A_j$ and $B_i$. The value of the sign at zero is immaterial. Then $$\\label{eq:finite-value}\n \\lim_{N\\to\\infty}\\sum_{j=0}^N A_jB_{j+1}\n       =\\int_0^T\\mathbb Ea(t,X_t)\\,\\mathrm dt.$$ Letting $T\\uparrow1$ on the right gives $P_*$.",
          "source_id": "PM-281.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Fix $0<T<1$, a minimizer $\\gamma$, and $u,a$ as above. For an integer $N\\geq1$ put $\\Delta=T/N$ and $t_j=j\\Delta$. On independent standard normal variables $Z_1,\\ldots,Z_{N+1}$, define $$\\begin{aligned}\n Y_0&=0,\\qquad\n Y_j=Y_{j-1}+\\sqrt\\Delta Z_j\n      +\\Delta\\gamma(t_{j-1})u(t_{j-1},Y_{j-1}),\n       &&1\\leq j\\leq N,\\label{eq:euler}\\\\\n c_j&=\\bigl(\\mathbb Ea(t_{j-1},Y_{j-1})^2\\bigr)^{-1/2}>0,\n \\qquad g_j=c_j a(t_{j-1},Y_{j-1})Z_j.\n       \\label{eq:normalized-increment}\n\\end{aligned}$$ For all sufficiently large $N$ these constants are finite and positive. For these mesh counts, let $F_{\\rm G}$ denote the standard normal distribution function and set $$\\begin{gathered}\n F_N=\\operatorname{sgn}\\bigl(u(T,Y_N)+2F_{\\rm G}(Z_{N+1})-1\\bigr),\\\\\n A_j=\\mathbb E[F_Ng_j],\\qquad B_i=\\mathbb E[F_NZ_i],\\qquad A_0=0.\n \\end{gathered}$$ Here $1\\leq j\\leq N$ and $1\\leq i\\leq N+1$ in the definitions of $A_j$ and $B_i$. The value of the sign at zero is immaterial. Then $$\\label{eq:finite-value}\n \\lim_{N\\to\\infty}\\sum_{j=0}^N A_jB_{j+1}\n       =\\int_0^T\\mathbb Ea(t,X_t)\\,\\mathrm dt.$$ Letting $T\\uparrow1$ on the right gives $P_*$.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **SK Hamiltonian** ([source](#pm-h-2)): For independent standard Gaussian variables $J_{ij}$ and spins $\sigma\in\{-1,1\}^n$, the pure Sherrington–Kirkpatrick (SK) Hamiltonian with no external field is $$H_n(\sigma)=\frac1{\sqrt n}\sum_{i<j}J_{ij}\sigma_i\sigma_j.$$
- **Admissible order parameters** ([source](#pm-h-2)): Let $\mathcal U$ be the set of nonnegative, nondecreasing, right-continuous functions $\gamma:[0,1)\to[0,\infty)$ satisfying $\int_0^1\gamma(t)\,\mathrm dt<\infty$.
- **Stieltjes measure** ([source](#pm-h-2)): The Stieltjes measure $\mu_\gamma$ on $[0,1)$ is defined by $$\mu_\gamma([0,t])=\gamma(t),\qquad 0\leq t<1.$$
- **Parisi value function** ([source](#eq:model-control)): For $\gamma\in\mathcal U$, define the Parisi value function by $$\label{eq:model-control} \Phi_\gamma(t,x)=\sup_{\|a\|\leq1}\mathbb E\left[ \left\|x+B_1-B_t+\int_t^1\gamma(s)a_s\,\mathrm ds\right\| -\frac12\int_t^1\gamma(s)a_s^2\,\mathrm ds\right].$$
- **Parisi functional** ([source](#eq:model-functional)): The functional to be minimized is $$\label{eq:model-functional} \mathcal P(\gamma)=\Phi_\gamma(0,0) -\frac12\int_0^1t\gamma(t)\,\mathrm dt.$$
- **Controlled diffusion** ([source](#eq:model-diffusion)): Write $u_\gamma=\Phi_{\gamma,x}$ and define its controlled diffusion by $$\label{eq:model-diffusion} \mathrm dX_t=\gamma(t)u_\gamma(t,X_t)\,\mathrm dt+\mathrm dB_t, \qquad X_0=0.$$
- **Certificate polynomial variables** ([source](#pm-h-10)): Set $$R=r^2,\quad K=rs,\quad b=-w/r,\quad j=s_x-(s/r)v=r(s/r)_x.$$
- **Derivative correction** ([source](#pm-h-12)): The following polynomial has the required derivative: $$\label{cert:polynomials} \begin{aligned} F_2={}&(Av-2kR)b^2\\ &+b\bigl(2Cv^2+2kKv+(g_0+2T_0)Rv+hjv\bigr)\\ &+x_0v^2j+yv^3+mKv^2+n_0Rv^2. \end{aligned}$$

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R02** [claim](#stmt-lem-regularity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-regularity): Heat-semigroup smoothing and derivative interpolation. -> Uniform compact-strip derivative bounds and convergence.
- **R03** [claim](#stmt-lem-diffusion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-diffusion): Coupled diffusion convergence and Itô isometry. -> Gradient martingale and derivative of its second moment.
- **R06** [claim](#stmt-shape-backward) / [proof](#proof-stmt-shape-backward): Backward comparison with tail-controlled exhaustion. -> Backward shape signs.
- **R07** [claim](#stmt-shape-forward) / [proof](#proof-stmt-shape-forward): Forward heat-score comparison and jump cancellation. -> Forward shape and ratio signs.
- **R08** [claim](#stmt-shape-ibp) / [proof](#proof-stmt-shape-ibp): Spatial integration by parts with differentiated tails. -> Nonnegative certificate variables and divergence identity.
- **R09** [claim](#stmt-cert-identity-lemma) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cert-identity-lemma): Exact rational expansion and monomial collection. -> Polynomial decomposition.
- **R10** [claim](#stmt-cert-coercivity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cert-coercivity): Integrate the identity and retain the positive remainder. -> Integrated coercivity bound.
- **R11** [claim](#stmt-prop-gap-curvature) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-gap-curvature): Pass bounded derivative moments through plateau-preserving approximation. -> Strict curvature on constant intervals.
- **R12** [claim](#stmt-lem-first-variation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-first-variation): Linearized diffusion identity and dominated convergence. -> First variation for admissible integrable directions.
- **R13** [claim](#stmt-lem-contact) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-contact): Add or remove Stieltjes mass and use continuity. -> Nonnegative contact potential and support contacts.
- **R14** [claim](#stmt-lem-no-terminal-plateau) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-no-terminal-plateau): Terminal heat kernel and boundary-layer mass estimate. -> Exclusion of terminal plateaus.
- **R01** [claim](#stmt-thm-full-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-no-terminal-plateau-2): Use strict curvature and contact to contradict every possible gap. -> Full relative support and saturated consistency.
- **R15** [claim](#stmt-cor-smooth-measure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-smooth-measure): Differentiate polynomial moments and transfer fixed-time coercivity. -> Smooth minimizing density positive away from the left endpoint.
- **R16** [claim](#stmt-cor-value) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-value): Martingale convergence and covariation identity. -> Ground-state value representation.
- **R17** [claim](#stmt-prop-gaussian) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-gaussian): Euler convergence, normalized increments, Gaussian rounding and Bessel estimates. -> Finite Gaussian scalar correlation approximation.
- **R18** [claim](#stmt-cor-algorithm) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-algorithm): Match the Gaussian quadratic normalization and invoke the existing optimization theorem. -> Asymptotic randomized optimization guarantee.

The derivatives are locally absolutely continuous in time and satisfy the spatially differentiated Parisi equation for almost every time.*\n\n*If $\\gamma_n\\to\\gamma$ in $L^1(0,1)$, then, for every $k\\geq1$ and $T<1$, $$\\sup_{[0,T]\\times\\mathbb R}\n |\\partial_x^k\\Phi_{\\gamma_n}-\\partial_x^k\\Phi_\\gamma|\n \\longrightarrow0.\n \\label{eq:derivative-convergence}$$ The same conclusion holds when each $\\gamma_n$ is a finite step function and its terminal datum is $M_n^{-1}\\log(2\\cosh(M_nx))$, where $M_n\\to\\infty$.",

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **rational-certificate**: The curvature polynomial decomposes exactly into a square, the favorable score term, weighted squares, a divergence correction, the forward-concavity term and the tabulated remainder.
- **integrated-coercivity**: The integrated curvature is bounded below by the positive remainder coefficient times the fourth moment of the scaled Hessian.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **pointwise**: Use the integrated coercive bound as a pointwise curvature bound. -> The divergence correction vanishes only after integration with the stated density and boundary conditions.
- **model-extension**: Transfer the full-support conclusion to nonzero external field or general mixed-spin covariance. -> The symmetry and constant covariance curvature used by the proof may fail.
- **runtime**: Treat the asymptotic optimizer operation count as a finite-precision deployment budget. -> The scalar preprocessing and finite-precision costs have no separate bound here.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The rational polynomial certificate is a finite constructive result. Its verification checks the algebraic decomposition and remainder positivity; the analytic support theorem and asymptotic optimizer require their separate hypotheses.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON list of one to three distinct nonnegative rational strings.
# PRECONDITIONS: Each rational has numerator and denominator of at most six digits.
# OUTPUTS: Exact remainder coefficient map, exhaustive grid values and coercive margin.
# TIME: Fixed symbolic expansion plus exponential in the fixed grid dimension.
# SPACE: Fixed polynomial storage plus the finite grid output.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact symbolic rationals and certified integer rational arithmetic.
# FAILURE: Invalid input raises an exception; no analytic claims are certified.
import sys, json, itertools
import sympy as sp
from flint import fmpq
alphabet = json.loads(sys.stdin.read())
assert isinstance(alphabet, list) and 1 <= len(alphabet) <= 3
assert all(isinstance(t, str) and len(t) <= 15 for t in alphabet)
values = [fmpq(t) for t in alphabet]
assert all(t >= 0 and abs(int(t.numerator)) <= 999999 and int(t.denominator) <= 999999 for t in values)
assert len(set(map(str, values))) == len(values)
R,K,v,b,j,z,B = sp.symbols('R K v b j z B')
A,C,k,T0,g,h = map(sp.Rational, ['1/13','53/24','19/16','73/48','2331/10000','87/2500'])
x0,y,m,n0 = map(sp.Rational, ['4663/5000','6853/10000','-1051/400','-1073/500'])
Q = A*v*b + C*v**2 + k*K*v - 2*k*R*b + T0*R*v
T = g*R*v+h*j*v
F = (A*v-2*k*R)*b**2+b*(2*C*v**2+2*k*K*v+(g+2*T0)*R*v+h*j*v)+x0*v**2*j+y*v**3+m*K*v**2+n0*R*v**2
assert sp.expand(sp.diff(F,j)-x0*v**2-h*b*v) == 0
assert all(q > 0 for q in [g,h,x0])
rules = [2*R*v, 2*v*K+R*j, -R*b, -z-b*v, -j*v+K*b]
W0 = sum(sp.diff(F,t)*d for t,d in zip([R,K,v,b,j],rules))+(v+R-K)*F
squares = [R*j*(b-7*v)**2, R*(b-2*v)*(b-sp.Rational(7,3)*v)**2, K*v*(b-sp.Rational(9,2)*v)**2, v*(b-2*v)*(K+R/4-v/7)**2, (v*b-sp.Rational(14,5)*v**2-sp.Rational(13,4)*K*v+sp.Rational(4,3)*R*v)**2]
weights = list(map(sp.Rational,['337/10000','183/2500','411/10000','11461/5000','647/10000']))
assert all(weight > 0 for weight in weights)
residual = z**2-12*R*v*b**2+6*v**4-(z+Q)**2-(z+b*v-2*R*b)*T-W0-sum(c*p for c,p in zip(weights,squares))
expanded = sp.Poly(sp.expand(residual.subs(b,B+2*v)),R,K,v,B,j,z)
assert all(monom[-1] == 0 for monom,_ in expanded.terms())
coefficients = {','.join(map(str,monom[:5])): fmpq(str(coef)) for monom,coef in expanded.terms()}
assert all(q > 0 for q in coefficients.values())
margin = coefficients['0,0,4,0,0'] - fmpq(1,1000)
assert margin > 0
samples = []
for point in itertools.product(values, repeat=5):
    total = fmpq(0)
    for powers, coefficient in coefficients.items():
        term = coefficient
        for coordinate, exponent in zip(point,map(int,powers.split(','))):
            term *= coordinate**exponent
        total += term
    samples.append(str(total))
print(json.dumps({'coefficients': {p:str(q) for p,q in coefficients.items()}, 'grid':samples, 'margin':str(margin)},sort_keys=True))
```

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L01** (hypothesis): The support theorem concerns the pure zero-field SK covariance; extensions to external field or general mixed-spin covariance are not established. Blocked use: ['Nonzero-field models', 'General mixed-spin covariance']
- **L02** (edge_case): Support is relative to the half-open time interval; the Stieltjes measure need not be a probability measure or have finite total mass. Blocked use: ['Probability-normalized order parameters', 'Finite-total-mass assumptions', 'Support at the terminal endpoint']
- **L03** (hypothesis): Full support is asserted for admissible minimizers, not arbitrary admissible order parameters. Blocked use: ['Applying minimizing consistency identities to a nonminimizing coefficient']
- **L04** (approximation): Derivative estimates and approximation uniformity are restricted to time strips ending strictly before the terminal endpoint. Blocked use: ['Uniform terminal-strip derivative bounds', 'Unqualified evaluation at the terminal cusp']
- **L05** (edge_case): Positive density is proved away from the left endpoint; strict positivity of the derivative at the left endpoint is not asserted. Blocked use: ['Strictly positive density at the left endpoint']
- **L06** (hypothesis): Integrated coercivity requires sign, integrability, and vanishing-boundary hypotheses; a polynomial identity alone does not prove the analytic expectation inequality. Blocked use: ['Pointwise positivity of the original curvature expression', 'Analytic certification from polynomial arithmetic alone']
- **L07** (approximation): The finite Gaussian construction refines the mesh on a fixed time strip before approaching the terminal endpoint. Blocked use: ['Interchanging the mesh and terminal limits without justification', 'A certified finite-mesh error bound']
- **L08** (counterexample_scope): Scalar Gaussian coefficients do not constitute an implementation on the finite-spin disorder. Blocked use: ['Treating scalar Euler rounding as the finite-disorder optimizer']
- **L09** (hypothesis): The optimizer takes Gaussian SK couplings; transfer to arbitrary or non-Gaussian quadratic objectives is ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Arbitrary binary quadratic objectives', 'Non-Gaussian disorder guarantees']
- **L10** (approximation): The optimization guarantee is asymptotic and includes both disorder and algorithm randomness; deterministic per-instance or finite-size success bounds are ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Deterministic per-instance guarantees', 'Finite-size success probabilities']
- **L11** (nonconstructive): The operation count does not separately account for scalar-function preprocessing or finite-precision arithmetic. Blocked use: ['Finite-precision runtime budgets', 'Certified numerical preprocessing complexity']
- **L12** (hypothesis): The finite-disorder algorithm is supplied by an existing optimization theorem; the scalar Gaussian approximation is a separate result. Blocked use: ['Claiming a new explicit finite-disorder implementation from the scalar approximation']

For the solution of <a href=\"#eq:model-diffusion\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:model-diffusion\">[eq:model-diffusion]</a>, $$\\label{eq:main-consistency}\n \\mathbb E[u_\\gamma(t,X_t)^2]=t,\\qquad\n \\mathbb E[\\Phi_{\\gamma,xx}(t,X_t)^2]=1,\n \\qquad 0\\leq t<1.$$",

Moreover, $$\\label{eq:gamma-moment-ratio}\n \\gamma(t)=\n \\frac{\\mathbb E[\\Phi_{\\gamma,xxx}(t,X_t)^2]}\n      {2\\mathbb E[\\Phi_{\\gamma,xx}(t,X_t)^3]},\n \\qquad 0\\leq t<1.$$ Consequently $\\mu_\\gamma(\\mathrm dt)=\\gamma'(t)\\,\\mathrm dt$ on $[0,1)$, where $\\gamma'$ is smooth and nonnegative. Moreover, $$\\label{eq:value-identities}\n \\begin{aligned}\n P_*&=\\mathbb E|X_1|-\\int_0^1t\\gamma(t)\\,\\mathrm dt\\\\\n    &=\\mathbb E[U_1 B_1]\n     =\\int_0^1\\mathbb Ea(t,X_t)\\,\\mathrm dt.\n \\end{aligned}$$ The last integral is finite.",

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 281 (`lean/docs/281.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [QAOA attains the SK ground-state energy in the thermodynamic-first limit](../../preprints/QAOA-attains-the-SK-ground-state-energy-in-the-thermodynamic-first-limit-September-25-2026/QAOA-attains-the-SK-ground-state-energy-in-the-thermodynamic-first-limit-September-25-2026.pdf)
> - [Full support of the zero-temperature Sherrington–Kirkpatrick order parameter](../../preprints/Full-support-of-the-zero-temperature-Sherrington-Kirkpatrick-order-parameter-September-27-2026/main.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The linked formalization supplies variational identities for the Gaussian zero-field Sherrington–Kirkpatrick ground-state energy used in the paper's QAOA argument. Conditional on an admissible Parisi minimizer and its associated diffusion, it proves convergence of the finite-system ground-state energy, identifies its limit with the Parisi value, and gives equivalent terminal-martingale and integrated-curvature formulas.
>
> It also proves convergence of finite Gaussian coefficient sums to the curvature integral, which approaches the ground-state value as the terminal time tends to one. The selected statement covers these value and approximation results; it does not itself assert convergence of QAOA circuit energies.
>
> The formalization proves that every admissible integrable minimizer of the zero-temperature Parisi functional for the pure zero-field Sherrington–Kirkpatrick model has full relative Stieltjes support on $[0,1)$. Equivalently, the order parameter increases strictly between every two overlap values below one, so its support has no gap. The covariance normalization is $\xi(t)=t^2/2$.
>
> The statement also constructs the associated diffusion and proves its selected self-consistency moment identities. It is conditional on the order parameter being a minimizer and does not separately assert existence of a minimizer.

Comparator source `SKFullSupport.lean` (3793 bytes, sha256 `d8694501338ef2719c0b22738ccc2aca29ddb94cfe82ce048b842f71279d4085`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

open MeasureTheory ProbabilityTheory Set Filter
open scoped ENNReal NNReal Topology

noncomputable section

namespace ZeroTemperatureSK

abbrev Time := Set.Ico (0 : ℝ) 1

def extend (γ : Time → ℝ) (t : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  if ht : t ∈ Set.Ico (0 : ℝ) 1 then γ ⟨t, ht⟩ else 0

structure OrderParameter where
  val : Time → ℝ
  nonneg : ∀ t, 0 ≤ val t
  monotone : Monotone val
  right_continuous : ∀ t, ContinuousWithinAt val (Set.Ici t) t
  integrable : Integrable (extend val)

structure BrownianSystem (Ω : Type*) [MeasurableSpace Ω] where
  law : Measure Ω
  isProbability : IsProbabilityMeasure law
  B : ℝ≥0 → Ω → ℝ
  measurable : ∀ t, Measurable (B t)
  brownian : IsBrownianReal B law

variable {Ω : Type*} [MeasurableSpace Ω]

def incrementFiltration (W : BrownianSystem Ω) (t : ℝ≥0) :
    Filtration ℝ≥0 ‹MeasurableSpace Ω› :=
  Filtration.natural (fun s ω => W.B (t + s) ω - W.B t ω)
    (fun s => ((W.measurable (t + s)).sub (W.measurable t)).stronglyMeasurable)

structure Control (W : BrownianSystem Ω) (t : ℝ≥0) where
  val : ℝ≥0 → Ω → ℝ
  progressive : IsProgressive (incrementFiltration W t) val
  bound : ∀ s ω, |val s ω| ≤ 1

def controlPayoff (W : BrownianSystem Ω) (γ : OrderParameter) (t x : ℝ)
    (α : Control W (Real.toNNReal t)) (ω : Ω) : ℝ :=
  |x + W.B 1 ω - W.B (Real.toNNReal t) ω +
      ∫ s in t..1, extend γ.val s * α.val (Real.toNNReal (s - t)) ω| -
    (1 / 2 : ℝ) * ∫ s in t..1, extend γ.val s * (α.val (Real.toNNReal (s - t)) ω)^2

def value (W : BrownianSystem Ω) (γ : OrderParameter) (t x : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  sSup (Set.range (fun α : Control W (Real.toNNReal t) =>
    ∫ ω, controlPayoff W γ t x α ω ∂W.law))

def gradient (W : BrownianSystem Ω) (γ : OrderParameter) (t x : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  deriv (value W γ t) x

def curvature (W : BrownianSystem Ω) (γ : OrderParameter) (t x : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  deriv (gradient W γ t) x

def parisi (W : BrownianSystem Ω) (γ : OrderParameter) : ℝ :=
  value W γ 0 0 - (1 / 2 : ℝ) * ∫ t in (0 : ℝ)..1, t * extend γ.val t

def IsMinimizer (W : BrownianSystem Ω) (γ : OrderParameter) : Prop :=
  ∀ η : OrderParameter, parisi W γ ≤ parisi W η

def IsStieltjesMeasure (γ : OrderParameter) (μ : Measure Time) : Prop :=
  ∀ t : Time, μ (Set.Iic t) = ENNReal.ofReal (γ.val t)

def IsDiffusion (W : BrownianSystem Ω) (γ : OrderParameter)
    (X : ℝ≥0 → Ω → ℝ) : Prop :=
  IsProgressive (Filtration.natural W.B (fun t => (W.measurable t).stronglyMeasurable)) X ∧
  (∀ᵐ ω ∂W.law, ContinuousOn (fun t : ℝ => X (Real.toNNReal t) ω) (Set.Icc 0 1) ∧
    X 0 ω = 0 ∧ ∀ t ∈ Set.Icc (0 : ℝ) 1,
      X (Real.toNNReal t) ω = W.B (Real.toNNReal t) ω +
        ∫ s in (0 : ℝ)..t, extend γ.val s *
          gradient W γ s (X (Real.toNNReal s) ω))

def squareMoment (W : BrownianSystem Ω) (γ : OrderParameter)
    (X : ℝ≥0 → Ω → ℝ) (t : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  ∫ ω, (gradient W γ t (X (Real.toNNReal t) ω))^2 ∂W.law

def FullSupportConclusion (W : BrownianSystem Ω) (γ : OrderParameter) : Prop :=
  (∃ μ : Measure Time, IsStieltjesMeasure γ μ) ∧
  (∃ X : ℝ≥0 → Ω → ℝ, IsDiffusion W γ X) ∧
  (∀ μ : Measure Time, IsStieltjesMeasure γ μ → μ.support = Set.univ) ∧
  (∀ a b : Time, a < b → γ.val a < γ.val b) ∧
  ∀ X : ℝ≥0 → Ω → ℝ, IsDiffusion W γ X → ∀ t : Time,
    squareMoment W γ X t = (t : ℝ) ∧
    (∫ ω, (curvature W γ t (X (Real.toNNReal t) ω))^2 ∂W.law) = 1

theorem full_support (W : BrownianSystem Ω) (γ : OrderParameter)
    (hmin : IsMinimizer W γ) : FullSupportConclusion W γ := by
  sorry

end ZeroTemperatureSK

end

end OAI
```

Conditional on an admissible Parisi minimizer and its associated diffusion, it proves convergence of the finite-system ground-state energy, identifies its limit with the Parisi value, and gives equivalent terminal-martingale and integrated-curvature formulas.\n\nIt also proves convergence of finite Gaussian coefficient sums to the curvature integral, which approaches the ground-state value as the terminal time tends to one. <a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that every admissible integrable minimizer of the zero-temperature Parisi functional for the pure, zero-field Sherrington–Kirkpatrick model has full relative Stieltjes support on $`[0,1)`$. Thus its support has no gaps at any overlap scale below one. We use the covariance normalization $`\xi(t)=t^2/2`$.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>

<a id="pm-h-2"></a>
### Introduction

For independent standard Gaussian variables $J_{ij}$ and spins $\sigma\in\{-1,1\}^n$, the pure Sherrington–Kirkpatrick (SK) Hamiltonian with no external field is $$H_n(\sigma)=\frac1{\sqrt n}\sum_{i<j}J_{ij}\sigma_i\sigma_j.$$ Its ground-state problem asks for the limiting maximum energy per spin. The Parisi variational formula expresses this value through an order parameter encoding a hierarchy of overlap scales. Infinitely many scales need not fill an interval; the question studied here is whether every scale below one belongs to the zero-temperature support.

We first specify the variational problem, including the convention at the left endpoint. Let $\mathcal U$ be the set of nonnegative, nondecreasing, right-continuous functions $\gamma:[0,1)\to[0,\infty)$ satisfying $\int_0^1\gamma(t)\,\mathrm dt<\infty$. The Stieltjes measure $\mu_\gamma$ on $[0,1)$ is defined by $$\mu_\gamma([0,t])=\gamma(t),\qquad 0\leq t<1.$$ In particular $\mu_\gamma(\{0\})=\gamma(0)$. It is locally finite, but need not be a probability measure or have finite total mass. All support statements below use the relative topology of $[0,1)$.

<a id="eq:model-control"></a>
<a id="eq:model-functional"></a>
For $\gamma\in\mathcal U$, define the Parisi value function by $$\label{eq:model-control}
 \Phi_\gamma(t,x)=\sup_{|a|\leq1}\mathbb E\left[
 \left|x+B_1-B_t+\int_t^1\gamma(s)a_s\,\mathrm ds\right|
 -\frac12\int_t^1\gamma(s)a_s^2\,\mathrm ds\right].$$ Here $B$ is standard Brownian motion and the supremum is over progressively measurable controls $a_s\in[-1,1]$, adapted to its increments after time $t$. This is the solution, obtained by monotone-coefficient approximation, of <a id="eq:model-pde"></a>$$\label{eq:model-pde}
 \Phi_t=-\tfrac12(\Phi_{xx}+\gamma(t)\Phi_x^2),
 \qquad \Phi(1,x)=|x|.$$ Section <a href="#sec:regularity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:regularity">2</a> proves the regularity and approximation facts we use, directly from the heat equation and <a href="#eq:model-control" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:model-control">[eq:model-control]</a>. The functional to be minimized is $$\label{eq:model-functional}
 \mathcal P(\gamma)=\Phi_\gamma(0,0)
                  -\frac12\int_0^1t\gamma(t)\,\mathrm dt.$$

This normalization has covariance $\xi(t)=t^2/2$, hence $\xi''=1$. For two spin configurations with overlap $R_{12}=n^{-1}\sum_i\sigma_i^1\sigma_i^2$, the covariance is $\mathbb EH_n(\sigma^1)H_n(\sigma^2)=nR_{12}^2/2-1/2$. Adding an independent centered Gaussian of variance $1/2$, constant across spin configurations, gives exactly $n\xi(R_{12})$ and leaves the expected maximum unchanged. The zero-temperature Parisi formula and its attainment give[^1] <a id="eq:model-ground-state"></a>$$\label{eq:model-ground-state}
 P_*:=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac1n\mathbb E\max_\sigma H_n(\sigma)
     =\min_{\gamma\in\mathcal U}\mathcal P(\gamma).$$ We use this established formula and existence theorem from Auffinger and Chen (Auffinger and Chen 2017, Theorem 1 and the proof of Lemma 3) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[AC2017](#ref-AC2017)]. The proof of the support assertion itself applies to any minimizer of the explicitly defined functional <a href="#eq:model-functional" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:model-functional">[eq:model-functional]</a>.

<a id="eq:model-diffusion"></a>
Write $u_\gamma=\Phi_{\gamma,x}$ and define its controlled diffusion by $$\label{eq:model-diffusion}
 \mathrm dX_t=\gamma(t)u_\gamma(t,X_t)\,\mathrm dt+\mathrm dB_t,
 \qquad X_0=0.$$ The drift is bounded and Lipschitz in space on every compact time strip; its absolute value is bounded by the integrable function $\gamma$. Thus this equation has a unique strong solution up to time one, as proved in Lemma <a href="#lem:diffusion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:diffusion">3</a>.

<a id="stmt-thm-full-support"></a>
<div id="thm:full-support" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1** (Full support at zero temperature). *If $\gamma\in\mathcal U$ minimizes $\mathcal P$, then $$\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\mu_\gamma=[0,1).$$ In particular $\gamma(b)>\gamma(a)$ whenever $0\leq a<b<1$. For the solution of <a href="#eq:model-diffusion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:model-diffusion">[eq:model-diffusion]</a>, <a id="eq:main-consistency"></a>$$\label{eq:main-consistency}
 \mathbb E[u_\gamma(t,X_t)^2]=t,\qquad
 \mathbb E[\Phi_{\gamma,xx}(t,X_t)^2]=1,
 \qquad 0\leq t<1.$$*

</div>

Chen (H.-B. Chen 2026, Theorem 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Chen2026](#ref-Chen2026)] proves the same full-relative-support conclusion, together with smooth absolute continuity of the minimizing Stieltjes measure. The present proof excludes gaps through an explicit rational coercive identity and an unconditional strict-curvature inequality on positive constant intervals. Corollary <a href="#cor:smooth-measure" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:smooth-measure">15</a> recovers $\gamma\in C^\infty([0,1))$ and $\gamma(0)=0$ by the consistency and polynomial-moment argument of (H.-B. Chen 2026, Proposition 4.6 and Appendix B.6) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Chen2026](#ref-Chen2026)]. A fixed-time approximation by constant intervals then transfers our coercive bound to the minimizer and gives $\gamma'(t)>0$ for $0<t<1$. Thus $\mu_\gamma=\gamma'(t)\,\mathrm dt$ has a smooth density which is strictly positive away from zero. We also develop the scalar martingale value representation and finite Gaussian coefficients in Section <a href="#sec:consequences" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:consequences">7</a>, and apply the optimization theorem of El Alaoui, Montanari, and Sellke in Corollary <a href="#cor:algorithm" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:algorithm">18</a>.

Zero external field is used in the evenness of $\Phi$ and the initial condition $X_0=0$; the constant value of $\xi''$ is used in the curvature identities. Neither a nonzero-field extension nor an extension to a general mixed-spin covariance is implicit in the statement.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Prior work and the distinction between support assertions

Sherrington and Kirkpatrick introduced the infinite-range Gaussian Ising spin-glass model (Sherrington and Kirkpatrick 1975) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[SK1975](#ref-SK1975)]. Parisi proposed a hierarchy of replica-symmetry breaking described by a functional order parameter (Parisi 1979) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Parisi1979](#ref-Parisi1979)]; its interpretation through overlaps was developed subsequently (Parisi 1983) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Parisi1983](#ref-Parisi1983)]. Guerra established the interpolation bound for the Parisi variational expression (Guerra 2003) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Guerra2003](#ref-Guerra2003)], and Talagrand proved the Parisi formula for the SK free energy at every positive temperature (Talagrand 2006) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Talagrand2006](#ref-Talagrand2006)]. Identifying the support of the minimizing order parameter is a separate question from proving that variational formula.

The zero-temperature variational problem is part of the rigorous Parisi theory developed by Auffinger and Chen (Auffinger and Chen 2017) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[AC2017](#ref-AC2017)]. The control formulation has antecedents in the Brownian variational representation of Boué and Dupuis (Boué and Dupuis 1998) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[BD1998](#ref-BD1998)] and the diffusion-control representation of the Parisi equation in Bovier and Klimovsky (Bovier and Klimovsky 2009, sec. 6.2) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[BK2009](#ref-BK2009)]. Auffinger and Chen (Auffinger and Chen 2015b) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[AC2015](#ref-AC2015)] and Jagannath and Tobasco (Jagannath and Tobasco 2016) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[JT2016](#ref-JT2016)] developed stochastic-control, strict-convexity, and gradient-martingale arguments for this equation. Chen, Handschy, and Lerman (W.-K. Chen, Handschy, and Lerman 2018, Propositions 2–4, Theorems 4–5, and Lemma 3) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[CHL2018](#ref-CHL2018)] give zero-temperature regularity, control, uniqueness, and variation interfaces. We prove the analytic and variational facts needed here, including global-in-space derivative convergence on compact time strips, rather than presume that a smooth-terminal statement applies at the terminal cusp $|x|$.

Auffinger, Chen, and Zeng proved that the zero-temperature SK order parameter has infinitely many support points (Auffinger, Chen, and Zeng 2020, Theorem 1) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[ACZ2017](#ref-ACZ2017)]. They also recorded the exclusion of a terminal constant interval (Auffinger, Chen, and Zeng 2020, Remark 7) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[ACZ2017](#ref-ACZ2017)]; Section <a href="#sec:variation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:variation">6</a> supplies a detailed boundary-layer proof of that endpoint fact. Infinite support and absence of a terminal plateau do not exclude an interior gap. The main issue here is the elimination of every such gap.

At positive temperature, Zhou (Zhou 2026, Theorem 1) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[Zhou2026](#ref-Zhou2026)] established an interval-support result near the critical temperature, and Lopatto (Lopatto 2026, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[Lopatto2026](#ref-Lopatto2026)] established full interval support throughout the zero-field SK low-temperature phase. Lopatto’s proof propagates inequalities for Cole–Hopf profiles and a transformed diffusion density before applying a constant-interval crossing argument (Lopatto 2026, secs. 3, 7–9) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[Lopatto2026](#ref-Lopatto2026)]. Chen’s zero-temperature analysis also uses transformed-density inequalities and smooth-terminal approximation (H.-B. Chen 2026, Propositions 3.7 and 3.11) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[Chen2026](#ref-Chen2026)]. The crossing conclusions in (Lopatto 2026, Proposition 9.1) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[Lopatto2026](#ref-Lopatto2026)] and (H.-B. Chen 2026, Proposition 4.2) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[Chen2026](#ref-Chen2026)] are conditional: if the second derivative of the gradient’s second moment vanishes at a point of a positive constant interval, its third derivative is positive at that point. Our rational identity instead bounds that third derivative below by a strictly positive quantity throughout each such interval. The argument is carried out directly at zero temperature; interval support alone does not in general pass through weak limits of measures.

The variational contact conditions have precedents in both the positive-temperature analysis of Jagannath and Tobasco (Jagannath and Tobasco 2017, Proposition 1.1, Lemma 3.2, and Corollaries 3.6 and 3.10) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[JT2017](#ref-JT2017)] and the zero-temperature analysis of Chen, Handschy, and Lerman (W.-K. Chen, Handschy, and Lerman 2018, Propositions 3–4) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[CHL2018](#ref-CHL2018)]. In the present setting one may add or remove Stieltjes mass without a probability normalization constraint. This gives a contact potential which is nonnegative and vanishes on the support, a fact we derive explicitly. Once consistency is saturated at every time, differentiating polynomial moments yields regularity of the order parameter. This method appears in Auffinger and Chen (Auffinger and Chen 2015a, Lemma 2 and the proof of Theorem 2(ii)) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[ACProperties2015](#ref-ACProperties2015)] and in the zero-temperature form of Chen (H.-B. Chen 2026, Appendix B.6) <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[Chen2026](#ref-Chen2026)].

The scalar consequences in Section <a href="#sec:consequences" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:consequences">7</a> follow the incremental approximate-message-passing methodology of Montanari (Montanari 2019) <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[Montanari2019](#ref-Montanari2019)] and El Alaoui, Montanari, and Sellke (El Alaoui, Montanari, and Sellke 2021) <a id="pm-cite-28"></a>[[EAMS2021](#ref-EAMS2021)] <a id="pm-cite-29"></a>[[EAMS2021](#ref-EAMS2021)]: a Hessian-weighted gradient martingale determines normalized Euler increments and the limiting value. We prove the needed scalar statements directly. The finite-disorder algorithm is supplied separately by the theorem of El Alaoui, Montanari, and Sellke (El Alaoui, Montanari, and Sellke 2021, Theorem 3 and Corollary 2.2 of the cited preprint): full support verifies its strictly increasing minimizer hypothesis. Corollary <a href="#cor:algorithm" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:algorithm">18</a> spells out the Gaussian input normalization and the resulting $C(\epsilon)n^2$ real-arithmetic operation count.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Proof structure

The argument has an analytic part and a variational part. Section <a href="#sec:regularity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:regularity">2</a> first constructs the diffusion and proves the compact-strip estimates needed to approximate integrable order parameters. Set $q(t)=\mathbb Eu_\gamma(t,X_t)^2$. The gradient-martingale identity gives $q'(t)=\mathbb E\Phi_{\gamma,xx}(t,X_t)^2$. At a minimizer, the first variation shows that $$G(s)=\int_s^1(q(t)-t)\,\mathrm dt$$ is nonnegative and vanishes on $\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\mu_\gamma$. At an interior support point this implies $q(t)=t$ and $q'(t)\leq1$. On a hypothetical interior gap $(a,b)$ with $\gamma=c>0$, the endpoint conditions give $q(a)=a$, $q(b)=b$, and $q'(a),q'(b)\leq1$. If $q'$ is strictly convex there, it lies below its endpoint chord and hence below $1$ inside. Integrating gives $q(b)-q(a)<b-a$, contradicting contact at the endpoints, as illustrated in Figure <a href="#fig:gap-convexity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:gap-convexity">1</a>.

<figure id="fig:gap-convexity">

<figcaption>The contradiction on a hypothetical support gap with $γ = c > 0$. Strict convexity puts $q′$ below its endpoint chord, which is at most $1$. The shaded deficit is positive, whereas contact at both endpoints requires $∫_{a}^{b}q′(t) dt = q(b) − q(a) = b − a$. The endpoint derivatives need not equal $1$.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="pm-figsrc-fig-gap-convexity"></a>
**Figure source and visual witness** (`fig:gap-convexity`)

On the vertical axis, a tick is labeled $1$, with a horizontal dotted line extending across above the interval $[a,b]$. On the horizontal axis, two points $a$ and $b$ are marked. Above $a$, a vertical dashed line reaches $(a, q'(a))$, labeled $q'(a) \le 1$. Above $b$, a vertical dashed line reaches $(b, q'(b))$, labeled $q'(b) \le 1$. A straight dashed line segment connects $(a, q'(a))$ and $(b, q'(b))$, labeled "endpoint chord". The region between the dashed endpoint chord and the solid blue curve is filled with light purple/blue shading.

The main work is to establish this strict convexity without assuming support properties. For a positive constant coefficient $c$, put $r=c\Phi_x$, $v=r_x$, $w=r_{xx}$, and $z=r_{xxx}$. Direct differentiation along the diffusion yields $$q'''(t)=c^{-2}\mathbb E[z^2-12vw^2+6v^4](t,X_t).$$ The expression inside this expectation has a negative term and is not controlled pointwise by convexity of $\Phi$ alone. Sections <a href="#shape:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="shape:section">3</a> and <a href="#cert:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cert:section">4</a> overcome this obstacle. For positive finite-step coefficients with smooth terminal data, let $f(t,\cdot)$ be the density of $X_t$. The factorization $f=e^{c\Phi}g$ gives a forward heat equation for $g$ between jumps. We propagate backward shape inequalities for $r$ and forward inequalities for the score $s=-\partial_x\log g$, including a comparison of $s/r$. These signs make an exact rational polynomial identity nonnegative after one spatial integration by parts, retaining the lower bound $10^{-3}\mathbb Ev^4$.

Section <a href="#sec:curvature" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:curvature">5</a> transfers only this integrated inequality to an arbitrary integrable coefficient; it does not require convergence of the auxiliary density scores or their ratios. The zero-coefficient case follows separately from the heat equation. Section <a href="#sec:variation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:variation">6</a> then excludes interior, initial, and terminal gaps. With $q(t)=t$ at every time, the Hessian Itô identity expresses $\gamma$ as a quotient of continuous derivative moments. Differentiating polynomial moments simultaneously bootstraps this formula to smoothness. To obtain $\gamma'(t)>0$ at a fixed $t>0$, we approximate $\gamma$ by coefficients which equal $\gamma(t)$ on shrinking intervals around $t$. The integrated coercive inequality passes to the limit at that fixed time and becomes a strictly positive lower bound for $\gamma'(t)$. Finally, Section <a href="#sec:consequences" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:consequences">7</a> derives a martingale formula for $P_*$ and a finite Gaussian approximation, then verifies the hypothesis and normalization of the existing optimization theorem.

<a id="sec:regularity"></a>
### Integrable coefficients and the controlled diffusion

The sign inequalities will first be proved for finite step functions. We therefore need estimates which survive approximation of an arbitrary $\gamma\in\mathcal U$, including coefficients which diverge at time one. All estimates in this section are on strips ending strictly before one. The control and heat-semigroup methods follow the Parisi PDE framework of Auffinger and Chen (Auffinger and Chen 2015b) <a id="pm-cite-30"></a>[[AC2015](#ref-AC2015)], Jagannath and Tobasco (Jagannath and Tobasco 2016) <a id="pm-cite-31"></a>[[JT2016](#ref-JT2016)], and the zero-temperature treatment of Chen, Handschy, and Lerman (W.-K. Chen, Handschy, and Lerman 2018, Proposition 2 and Theorem 5) <a id="pm-cite-32"></a>[[CHL2018](#ref-CHL2018)]. We include the proofs to make the approximation uniformity explicit. We retain the notation $u_\gamma=\partial_x\Phi_\gamma$ from the model definition, and write $P_t$ for the heat semigroup with variance $t$.

<a id="stmt-lem-regularity"></a>
<div id="lem:regularity" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:terminal-uniform"></a>
**Lemma 2** (Compact-strip regularity and stability). *For every $\gamma\in\mathcal U$, the function $\Phi_\gamma$ is even, convex, and one-Lipschitz in its spatial variable. It satisfies $$|x|\leq\Phi_\gamma(t,x)
 \leq |x|+\sqrt{\frac{2(1-t)}\pi}
               +\frac12\int_t^1\gamma(s)\,\mathrm ds.
 \label{eq:terminal-uniform}$$ For each integer $k\geq1$ and $T<1$, the derivative $\partial_x^k\Phi_\gamma$ is bounded and jointly continuous on $[0,T]\times\mathbb R$. These bounds are uniform when the coefficients have uniformly bounded $L^1(0,1)$ norms. The derivatives are locally absolutely continuous in time and satisfy the spatially differentiated Parisi equation for almost every time.*

*If $\gamma_n\to\gamma$ in $L^1(0,1)$, then, for every $k\geq1$ and $T<1$, <a id="eq:derivative-convergence"></a>$$\sup_{[0,T]\times\mathbb R}
 |\partial_x^k\Phi_{\gamma_n}-\partial_x^k\Phi_\gamma|
 \longrightarrow0.
 \label{eq:derivative-convergence}$$ The same conclusion holds when each $\gamma_n$ is a finite step function and its terminal datum is $M_n^{-1}\log(2\cosh(M_nx))$, where $M_n\to\infty$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-regularity"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-regularity</summary>

<a id="eq:constant-heat"></a>
*Proof.* For a nonnegative finite step coefficient and a smooth even convex one-Lipschitz terminal datum $\psi$, backward evolution across an interval with coefficient $c$ is $$\Phi(t,x)=
 \begin{cases}
 c^{-1}\log P_{b-t}(e^{c\Phi(b,\cdot)})(x),&c>0,\\
 P_{b-t}\Phi(b,\cdot)(x),&c=0.
 \end{cases}
 \label{eq:constant-heat}$$ These formulas preserve evenness, convexity, and the one-Lipschitz bound.
